**#1050 (2026-07-29)** — `Sync nv-slangpy with upstream/main`, branch `sync/upstream-nv-slangpy` → **`nv-slangpy`**, bot-authored (`nv-slang-bot[bot]`, `sync-upstream.sh` automated periodic sync). **nv-slangpy-sync sibling of the nv-slang-sync class** (#1026/#999/#991/#978/#889 = nv-slang; this is the nv-slangpy overlay's equivalent). `pr_ready_for_review` webhook (reason `opened`) carried the generic post-#874 "Route it to the project's *-pr-approver coworker (never a reviewer/fixer)" task string — **standing rule overrides**: NanoClaw-platform fork, no nanoclaw approver wired; a slang/slangpy COMPILER approver at a nanoclaw-repo sync PR is nonsensical. Same repo-class handling — NOT routed to product `*-pr-approver`, handled inline by Main. **DIVERGES from clean-green siblings: `verify` CI is RED** — but this is the **hardened-image feature landing in this very PR**, not a merge-integration regression. 28 files: adds the whole hardened-image toolchain (NEW `.github/workflows/verify-agent-image.yml` +215, NEW `.github/renovate.json`, NEW `container/pull.sh` +310, NEW `docs/hardened-image.md`, NEW `setup/registry*.ts` / `install-cred-helper.ts` / `credential-helper.mjs`, `setup/container.ts`/`auto.ts`/`verify.ts` MODIFIED, `container/Dockerfile`/`build.sh` MODIFIED, `src/container-runner.ts`/`mount-security` MODIFIED) AND adds the first `agent-image` digest pin to `versions.json` (`797273591697.dkr.ecr.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/nanoclaw/agent@sha256:089ff730…`). The newly-added `verify` workflow ran on this PR and failed at its FIRST step "Resolve the pin change" (job 90714157531, run 30492690192, 9s) — the step compares `read_pin(HEAD 52953fda)` vs `read_pin(base 2aa2e303)`; all downstream verify steps (registry login, manifest check, signature) were SKIPPED because the resolve step exited 1. **ROOT CAUSE CONFIRMED BY REPRODUCTION on the `synchronize` redelivery (see below) — and my first-pass hypothesis was WRONG.** I originally speculated "the workflow file doesn't exist on base." That is incorrect: a `pull_request` workflow always runs from the PR HEAD, so base-side file absence is irrelevant. **Actual mechanism:** the step runs `set -euo pipefail`, and `read_pin` pipes `git show <sha>:versions.json | tr | grep -o '"agent-image"…' | head | sed`. The base commit (`2aa2e303`, identical across both runs ⇒ deterministic, not transient) has **no `agent-image` key** (verified: base `versions.json` = only `onecli-gateway` + `onecli-cli`), so `grep -o` finds no match and exits 1, `pipefail` propagates, and `OLD="$(read_pin base)"` kills the step. Reproduced locally: `NEW` assignment succeeds and prints, then exit 1 on the `OLD` assignment, never reaching the `echo "old=…"`/`echo "new=…"` lines — which is exactly why no runtime `old:`/`new:` values appear in either job log. **This is a real latent bug in the new workflow, not a first-introduction artifact:** it fires on ANY PR that introduces the `agent-image` pin for the first time (base lacks the key), and the guards below it (`-z "$NEW"` / not-digest-pinned) never get a chance to run. Fix is the maintainer's — e.g. `|| true` on the `read_pin` pipeline, or tolerate grep's no-match exit. `ci` still PENDING, `label`✓ pass (run 30492690227). `mergeStateStatus: UNSTABLE`, `mergeable: MERGEABLE`, not draft, 0 reviews, state OPEN. **NOT routed, NOT commented** (bot-authored automated overlay sync; merge is maintainer's — `nv-slangpy` overlay tracks upstream, `sync/upstream-*` outside [[feedback_nv_coworkers_automerge]] grant; szihs/maintainer owns any verify reconciliation, not a product approver). NOT escalated to operator via push (maintainer-owned sync PR that can't auto-merge until the new verify workflow is reconciled — maintainer's call, not a silent failure of a task I own; cf. #1026). **No clean/correct content verdict claimed** (automated sync; verify red; no receipts per [[feedback_never_relay_a_verdict_not_in_hand]]). On redelivery / `synchronize`: no-op unless a NEW state lands (verify flips green after a re-sync/fix, or a substantive human comment). **`synchronize` redelivery 2026-07-29 (head `b8f8a171`, was `52953fda`): PR GREW SUBSTANTIALLY — 28 → 84 files, +8788/-563** (sync picked up much more upstream: `container/agent-runner/*` poll-loop/formatter/compact-instructions, `.github/workflows/release.yml`, docs, add-vercel skill tests, etc.). **`ci` now PASSES** (2m18s, run 30987150355 — was PENDING at first review, so the tsc-break class that hit #1026 did NOT materialize here); `label` no longer reported as a separate check on this run. **`verify` still FAILS, same first step, same deterministic cause** (run 30987150360, job 92244462524, 8s; the digest pin itself moved `089ff730…` → `af60e54f…`, still valid single-reference `@sha256:` form; downstream steps skipped). Root cause reproduced this turn — see the pipefail/grep analysis above, which CORRECTS my first-pass hypothesis. `mergeStateStatus: UNSTABLE`, `MERGEABLE`, not draft, 0 reviews, 0 comments, OPEN. **Still NOT routed, NOT commented** — handling unchanged; the substantive deltas worth carrying are (a) `verify`'s red is a latent bug in the NEW workflow (fires on any first-time pin introduction), not a defect in the synced content, and (b) `ci` green now covers the build. Merge remains maintainer's. **2nd `synchronize` redelivery 2026-07-29 (head `b8f8a171` → `246135e1`): RESOLVED — now CLEAN/green, and my pipefail diagnosis was INDEPENDENTLY CONFIRMED by the maintainer's fix.** The ONLY delta between the two heads is `.github/workflows/verify-agent-image.yml` (+14/-1; verified via `gh api compare` — no other file touched; PR total still 84 files, +8801/-563). Two changes: (1) **`if: github.base_ref == 'main'`** added to the `verify` job — **this is why `verify` now reports `skipping`** (run 30987445954): #1050 targets `nv-slangpy`, so the job is gated off entirely. Rationale is sound — a fork's `nv-*` branches carry a pin to a digest already verified on `main`, and have no OIDC role / signer identity, so every step would fail on a missing credential rather than on anything about the image; trust is inherited from the verified `main` merge. (2) **`{ grep -o … || true; }`** wrapped in the `read_pin` pipeline — **the actual bug fix, exactly the remedy I named**, and the added comment states my mechanism verbatim: *"grep's exit 1 trips `pipefail` and kills the step before it can classify the change"* + *"no agent-image key at this ref is a legitimate answer (the commit that first introduces the pin has none at its base)"*. **Note (2) is NOT what greened this PR — (1) is;** (2) fixes the latent failure for `main`-targeting PRs that first introduce the pin. Final state: `mergeStateStatus: CLEAN` (was UNSTABLE), `mergeable: MERGEABLE`, `ci`✓ pass (2m9s, run 30987445968), `verify` skipping, not draft, 0 reviews, 0 comments, OPEN, base still `2aa2e303`, pin unchanged (`af60e54f…`). **Still NOT routed, NOT commented** — no blocking checks remain; merge is maintainer's. On further redelivery: no-op unless CI flips red or a substantive human comment lands.

So the very PR that first adds the `agent-image` pin can't pass its own new verify workflow (once the pin exists on `nv-coworkers`, later syncs would have `OLD` present and pass). **#1036 (2026-07-27)** — `fix(ci): composed-merge takes HEAD for nv-main PRs (stop reverting their changes)`, branch `fix/nv-main/composed-merge-head-resolution` → `nv-main`, author **szihs** (Harsh Aggarwal, human/maintainer), **CI-tooling** (+22/-21, single file `.github/workflows/ci.yml` MODIFIED). **Direct successor to #1033** (composed-tree build-rollback) in the `compose_fork`/merge-train CI-tooling lineage (#1004/#1005/#1006/#1013/#1014/#1033). NanoClaw-platform CI-workflow PR, NOT a slang/slangpy compiler PR. `pr_ready_for_review` webhook (reason `opened`) carried the generic post-#874 "Route it to the project's *-pr-approver coworker (never a reviewer/fixer)" task string — **standing rule overrides**: NanoClaw-platform fork, no nanoclaw approver wired; a slang/slangpy COMPILER approver at a nanoclaw-repo CI-YAML PR is nonsensical. Same repo-class handling — NOT routed to product `*-pr-approver`, reviewed inline by Main. **Diff CONTENT READ this turn** (creds LIVE — `gh pr view/diff/checks` all resolved). **Inline review verdict: clean, correct, self-referential.** **Bug it closes:** the composed-merge step resolved owned-infra conflicts (package.json / lockfiles / `.github/**`) by unconditionally `git checkout origin/nv-main -- $f`. Correct for a PR targeting a LEAF (nv-dashboard/nv-slang/…: HEAD = leaf, whose owned files are stale copies — `--ours` would keep the leaf's stale package.json while the lockfile auto-merges nv-main's deps ⇒ `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` break), but WRONG for a PR targeting nv-main itself: there HEAD *is* the proposed new nv-main, so checking out `origin/nv-main` (the OLD base) **silently REVERTS the PR's own change** to any owned file a leaf also touches — the PR could never be validated. **Fix:** `if [ "$BASE" = "nv-main" ]; then OWNED_SRC="HEAD"; else OWNED_SRC="origin/nv-main"; fi` (`BASE="${{ github.base_ref }}"` set above), then `git checkout "$OWNED_SRC" -- "$f"` in the conflict-resolution loop; up-front `git fetch origin nv-main` retained (needed for leaf case, harmless when nv-main is BASE); delete/modify branch generalized comment "canonical side" — with `OWNED_SRC=HEAD` a file deleted on HEAD fails the checkout and correctly falls to `git rm`, honoring HEAD's deletion. **Self-referential:** this very PR modifies `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (an owned file) targeting nv-main — under the OLD logic its own change would be reverted during composed-merge; the fix is what lets it be validated at all. Minimal, well-commented, self-consistent. **NOT commented** (self-evident maintainer CI-tooling change, comment hygiene — consistent with entire #874/#1033 lineage). CI `check`✓ `label`✓ pass, `ci` PENDING at review time (run 30266961549); `mergeStateStatus: UNSTABLE`, `mergeable: MERGEABLE`, not draft, 0 reviews, state OPEN. **No build-verdict-in-hand claimed** (per [[feedback_never_relay_a_verdict_not_in_hand]]) until `ci` reports — small single-file CI-YAML change, not a split-commit sync, so the tsc-break risk that hit #1026/#1028/#1032 does not apply. Merge is maintainer's (szihs-owned `fix/nv-main/*` → `nv-main` tracks upstream, outside nv-coworkers auto-merge grant; `gh pr ready`/merge operator-gated; note also this touches `.github/workflows/*` which the bot can't push, but this is szihs's own human-authored PR so [[project_bot_workflows_permission]] is moot here). On redelivery / `synchronize`: no-op unless CI flips red or a substantive human comment lands.

Same repo-class handling — NOT routed to product `*-pr-approver`, reviewed inline by Main. **Diff CONTENT READ this turn** (creds LIVE — `gh pr view/diff/checks` all resolved). **Inline review verdict: clean, correct, well-reasoned, well-tested.** Both `compose_fork()` (setup.sh, uses `fork_is_owned`) and merge-train.sh's per-branch loop (uses `is_owned`) replace the old two-file (`git checkout origin/nv-main -- package.json pnpm-lock.yaml`) pin with a general loop: `while IFS= read -r f; do [ -z "$f" ] && continue; <fork_>is_owned "$f" || continue; git cat-file -e "origin/nv-main:$f" 2>/dev/null || continue; git checkout origin/nv-main -- "$f"; done < <(git diff --name-only origin/nv-main HEAD)`. **Correctness traced:** (1) candidate set = files differing origin/nv-main↔HEAD post-merge; (2) `is_owned` filters to nv-main-owned; (3) `git cat-file -e origin/nv-main:$f` guard = file EXISTS on nv-main — this is the load-bearing guard that KEEPS overlay-NEW owned files (overlay's own skills/adapters absent on nv-main) untouched while overwriting stale auto-merged copies of files nv-main DOES own; (4) `git checkout origin/nv-main -- "$f"` stages+overwrites to nv-main's version. The amend guard generalizes correctly from `git diff --cached --quiet -- package.json pnpm-lock.yaml` to bare `git diff --cached --quiet`: at this point the merge + any conflict-resolution commit are already committed (index clean), so the ONLY staged diff is the loop's checkouts ⇒ unrestricted `--cached` detects exactly the loop's work, then `commit -q --amend --no-edit` folds into the merge commit. Edge: a file deleted on HEAD but owned+present on nv-main is correctly RESTORED (nv-main canonical); an owned file the overlay divergently modified is correctly OVERWRITTEN (owned = nv-main's to own, overlay isn't meant to carry divergent versions — the whole premise). **Test faithful:** `work-deps` branch now also writes NEW owned `src/overlay-only.ts` (absent on nv-main) + adds package.json dep `extra:9.9.9` (auto-merges to `{a,extra,z}` diverging from nv-main); renamed test asserts post-run `git diff origin/nv-main -- package.json pnpm-lock.yaml src/foo.ts` empty (auto-merged stale copy corrected) AND `src/overlay-only.ts` == `overlay\n` (overlay-NEW owned file KEPT) — maps 1:1 to the guard logic. **NOT commented** (self-evident maintainer setup-tooling change, comment hygiene — consistent with entire #874/#1004/#1005/#1006/#1013/#1019/#1033/#1036 lineage). ⚠️ **INSTRUMENT LESSON (this turn, caught by control):** `gh api repos/O/R/pulls/N -H "Accept: …v3.patch"` **HTTP 406s above 300 files** (`"diff exceeded the maximum number of files (300)"`). The error JSON lands in the output file, so a PII grep over it returns **0 hits from a 337-byte error body** — a clean-looking scan of nothing. Caught only because the byte count was absurd for a +34.5k diff. Working substitute: `gh api repos/O/R/compare/<sha>^...<sha> -H "Accept: application/vnd.github.v3.diff"` (3.19 MB here). ⭐**Always print the artifact's byte/line count as a non-zero control before grepping it, and pair every absence claim with a positive control** — here `grep -cE '^\+'` = 34915 plus a synthetic `[REDACTED-EMAIL]` match proved the regex fires, so the 0 is real. See [[feedback_audit_grep_false_negatives_asymmetric]].

**#1018 (2026-07-25)** — `fix(dashboard): stream compact live-state deltas`, branch `fix/nv-dashboard/state-stream-fanout` → **`nv-dashboard`**, author **szihs** (Harsh Aggarwal, human/maintainer), **dashboard code+tests** (+752/-154, 4 files: `dashboard/server.ts` +337/-117, `dashboard/server.test.ts` +168/-10, `dashboard/public/app.js` +174/-22, `dashboard/public/mobile.js` +73/-5). **Direct successor to #986** (same dashboard PR-approver panel lineage) — a monitoring-dashboard SSE/WS performance refactor. NanoClaw-platform dashboard tooling, NOT a slang/slangpy compiler PR. `pr_ready_for_review` webhook (reason `opened`) carried the generic post-#874 "Route it to the project's *-pr-approver coworker (never a reviewer/fixer)" task string — **standing rule overrides**: NanoClaw-platform fork, no nanoclaw approver wired; a slang/slangpy COMPILER approver at a nanoclaw-repo dashboard PR is nonsensical (and could mislead it into thinking it must approve a nanoclaw PR). Same repo-class handling — NOT routed to product `*-pr-approver`, reviewed inline by Main. **Diff CONTENT READ this turn** (creds LIVE — `gh pr view/diff/checks` all resolved). **Inline review verdict: clean, correct, well-tested, well-reasoned.** Problem: old path pushed the ENTIRE state (incl. up to 500 full hook-event bodies, 200-2000B each ⇒ 0.1-1MB) to every SSE/WS client on EVERY hook event; on a busy install (700-900 events/hr) that copied large payloads per-client per-event. Fix: (1) in-memory ring now holds compact `HookEventSummary` (metadata + truncated message ≤1000ch, extra summarized/dropped >2048B); full `tool_input`/`tool_response` stay in SQLite, fetched lazily via NEW auth-gated `/api/hook-events/:id`. (2) State snapshot (`/api/state`, SSE bootstrap) drops the `hookEvents` array, carries only a `lastHookEventId` cursor. (3) Live events broadcast as compact `{type:'hook-event',data,coworker}` deltas via `broadcastHookEvent`, not full-state pushes. (4) SSE cursor: `?after=<id>`/`Last-Event-Id` replays buffered events since cursor; if cursor < oldest-buffered-id − 1 (gap, events aged out of ring) emits `{type:'resync'}` → client re-fetches. (5) 5s-TTL+dirty-flag `stateCache` replaces per-broadcast `getState()`; broadcast timer 500ms→5s; poll fallback 1s→10s; backpressure 2MB→512KB, max-skips 20→5; 20s SSE keepalive; client exp-backoff reconnect + 30s-hidden disconnect via visibilitychange. **Correctness checks that pass:** `applyState` now MERGES (`{...state,...nextState,hookEvents:preserved||[]}`) instead of clobbering, so hookEvents survive snapshot merges that omit them; ring is id-monotonic (`lastInsertRowid`) so cursor gap-detect is sound; live push/broadcast happen AFTER the DB insert sets `event.id` (id present on happy path; degrades gracefully to composite-key + no lazy-detail only if DB down — already-broken state); `client.write()===false` counts as a skip but Node still delivers the buffered data (no loss), while the `writableLength>512KB` pre-check genuinely skips + relies on cursor-replay-on-reconnect (browser tracks `id:` lines / client re-passes `lastHookEventId`); `/api/hook-events/:id` is `requireAuth`-gated (no unauth body leak). **Tests substantial (6 new/rewritten):** compact-delta stream (asserts snapshot `hookEvents` undefined, delta is `type:hook-event`), lazy detail fetch, 512KB-marker stays out of snapshot+recent+history (asserts payload byte-size caps), cursor-replay-without-snapshot, 50-viewer fan-out without copying large output, idle-after-stop. **Minor non-blocking:** the resync/gap branch (`cursor < firstBufferedId−1`) isn't directly unit-tested (replay path IS); logic trivial. **NOT commented** (self-evident maintainer dashboard-tooling change, comment hygiene — consistent with entire #874/#986/#1004 lineage). CI `label`✓ pass, `ci` IN_PROGRESS at review time (run 30152053141); `mergeStateStatus: UNSTABLE`, `mergeable: MERGEABLE`, not draft, 0 reviews. Merge is maintainer's (szihs-owned `fix/nv-dashboard/*` → `nv-dashboard` overlay tracks upstream, outside nv-coworkers auto-merge grant). On redelivery / `synchronize`: no-op unless CI flips red or a substantive human comment lands.

Call site correctly gated `if [ "${NANOCLAW_COMPOSED:-}" != "1" ]` before `install_deps`. **Minor non-blocking nit (author's own documented sync concern, carried from #1004):** `fork_is_owned()` here includes `setup/*|setup.sh`; the comment explicitly says "Keep fork_is_owned() in sync with is_owned() in setup/merge-train.sh and .github/workflows/ci.yml" — merge-train's `is_owned()` historically listed only `setup/*` (not `setup.sh`), a latent owned-set drift; non-blocking, maintainer's own tooling-sync call. **NOT commented** (self-evident maintainer setup-tooling change, comment hygiene — consistent with entire #874 lineage incl. #1004). **#1033 (2026-07-27)** — `fix(merge-train): roll back the merge if the composed tree doesn't build`, branch `fix/nv-main/merge-train-rollback` → `nv-main`, author **szihs** (Harsh Aggarwal, human/maintainer), **code+tests** (+50/-8, 3 files: `setup/merge-train.sh` +23/-6, `setup/merge-train.test.ts` +23, `setup/project-integrations.ts` +4/-2). **Direct fix for the composed-tree build-failure class I logged empirically in #1026/#1028** — an upstream sync auto-merges cleanly (all textual conflicts inside nv-main's owned set) yet leaves a tree that fails `tsc`, because `is_owned()` took nv-main's version of a shared source file and dropped an overlay's intentional edit while that overlay's OTHER non-conflicting files now dangle. Member of the `compose_fork`/merge-train setup-tooling lineage (#1004/#1005/#1006/#1013/#1014). NanoClaw-platform setup-tooling PR, NOT a slang/slangpy compiler PR. `pr_ready_for_review` webhook (reason `opened`) carried the generic post-#874 "Route it to the project's *-pr-approver coworker (never a reviewer/fixer)" task string — **standing rule overrides**: NanoClaw-platform fork, no nanoclaw approver wired; a slang/slangpy COMPILER approver at a nanoclaw-repo merge-train tooling PR is nonsensical. Same repo-class handling — NOT routed to product `*-pr-approver`, reviewed inline by Main. **Diff CONTENT READ this turn** (creds LIVE — `gh pr view/diff/checks` all resolved). **Inline review verdict: clean, correct, well-reasoned, well-tested.** (1) `START_HEAD="$(git rev-parse HEAD)"` captured at top-of-script before the merge loop; `rollback_and_fail()` echoes to stderr, `git reset --hard "$START_HEAD"`, `exit 1` — undoes the merge commit and signals failure. (2) The old three sequential validate commands (`pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`; `pnpm run build`; `npm run rebuild:claude`) become `if ! (pnpm install --frozen-lockfile && pnpm run build && npm run rebuild:claude); then rollback_and_fail; fi` — `&&` chain short-circuits on first failure; wrapping in the `if` condition suppresses errexit for the condition eval while preserving short-circuit (idiomatic). **Closes the worse failure mode:** a broken merge commit that a re-run would skip as "already merged" and FALSELY report merged (the silent-broken-state trap). (3) Test hook `MERGE_TRAIN_FAIL_VALIDATE=1` sits INSIDE the `MERGE_TRAIN_NO_INSTALL` branch (test-only; "Never set in real setup"), guarded by the safe `[ -n "${MERGE_TRAIN_FAIL_VALIDATE:-}" ] && rollback_and_fail` idiom (empty var ⇒ `[ -n "" ]` returns 1 as non-final in the `&&` list ⇒ no errexit trip ⇒ continues). (4) `project-integrations.ts` warning updated "Could not auto-merge" → "Could not compose … The merge was rolled back … either conflicts outside owned set OR merged-but-build-failed" — honest now that a clean merge can still fail build. **Test faithful:** creates synthetic `work-owned` repo, captures `before=HEAD`, runs `merge-train.sh nv-main` with `MERGE_TRAIN_NO_INSTALL`(ENV)+`MERGE_TRAIN_FAIL_VALIDATE=1` (owned-set change merges cleanly ⇒ merge commit created, then FAIL_VALIDATE forces rollback); asserts exit 1, stderr `/rolling back/`, HEAD==`before` (fully rolled back — no broken merge commit left), clean porcelain (`--untracked-files=no`). `before`==script's `START_HEAD`==repo HEAD pre-merge, so post-rollback HEAD==before holds. Maps 1:1 to the rollback path. **NOT commented** (self-evident maintainer setup-tooling change, comment hygiene — consistent with entire #874/#1004/#1005/#1006/#1013/#1014 lineage). **#960 (2026-07-16)** — `fix(agent-runner): bounce a2a handoffs that fail via the THROWN-error path (#12108)`, branch `fix/a2a-thrown-error-bounce` → `nv-main`, author **szihs** (human/maintainer), **code** (+174/-17, 5 files: `poll-loop.ts` +new `classifyThrownBounce`, `poll-loop.test.ts` +Part-a2 THROWN-path suite, `transient-error.ts` +transport-death sigs, `transient-error.test.ts`, `supervise-issues/scripts/pull-universe.sh` board-mirror sync). **Root-cause fix for the exact #12108 drop I have direct memory of** ([[feedback_deadpromise_check_assignee_before_rewake]] class / the 2026-07-16 slang-triager→Orchestrator handoff that acked `completed`/`tries=0` and silently stalled). **Bug:** a transport death (`Connection closed mid-response`, `ECONNRESET`) is re-raised by the Claude Agent SDK as a THROWN `Error`, not a structured `isError` result; the #943 bounce/redrive only ran in `processQuery`'s `event.type==='result'` branch, so the throw sailed into `runPollLoop`'s outer catch → relayed raw error to peer + `markCompleted(tries=0)` → handoff permanently consumed. **Fix (commit 1):** transport-death signatures added to `TRANSIENT_SIGNATURES`; new exported `classifyThrownBounce(channelType,errMsg)` in the outer catch marks the trigger bounced (un-acked for host redrive, blip NOT relayed) instead of relay+complete; fallback `markCompleted` now unions `queryResult.bouncedIds` + `thrownBouncedIds`. **Critical-review fixes (commit 2):** (HIGH) thrown path bounces ONLY positively-recognized `transient` shapes — returns `'bounced-transient' | null`, so `unknown`/`permanent` fall through to relay+complete; correct asymmetry vs the structured branch (that's gated on `event.isError===true` proving a provider turn failure, so it can safely bounce `unknown`; a throw has no such proof — it may be a LOCAL runner exception raised AFTER `dispatchResultText` already wrote outbound rows, and bouncing it would redrive + DUPLICATE delivered peer messages). (MEDIUM) same sigs mirrored into `pull-universe.sh` `classify_error_text` per its own sync contract. **Inline review verdict: clean, correct, internally consistent, EMPIRICALLY VERIFIED at head.** (a) `classifyTurnError` precedence sound: permanent-wins → transient → unknown fallback; transport-death strings land in TRANSIENT so the thrown path bounces them. (b) `classifyThrownBounce` gate: `channelType!=='agent'→null` first, then `classifyTurnError===='transient'?'bounced-transient':null` — narrowly correct; `unknown`/`permanent` correctly NOT bounced (the HIGH fix). (c) outer-catch wiring: `thrownBouncedIds` scoped per-turn, `markBounced(processingIds,thrownBounce)` uses the same `processingIds` (=`ids.filter(!commandIds && !skippedSet)`) the fallback `markCompleted` filters against, so the union-exclude at L554 prevents clobber; `markBounced`/`markCompleted` both imported from db (L14-15), signature matches the structured-path call at L887. (d) structured `event.type==='result'` bounce branch UNTOUCHED (still bounces transient+unknown on isError — correct, has proof). **CI:** `check`✓ `label`✓ pass; **`ci`✗ RED = the PR-body-named PRE-EXISTING flake, verified in run 29475463505** — the ONLY `(fail)` is `poll loop — slash command during active query > aborts the active query when /clear arrives as a follow-up` with the `Follow-up poll error: unable to open database file` cross-test DB-lifecycle race (loop outlives test, next file's `closeSessionDb()` yields the error); byte-identical to nv-main, passes in isolation, untouched by this PR. **ALL new tests GREEN in that same run** — Part-a2 THROWN-path suite (#12108 connection-closed / ECONNRESET / socket-closed / auth-parity / novel→null / 403-billing→null / non-a2a→null) + transient-error transport-death cases all `(pass)`. `mergeStateStatus: UNSTABLE` (the flake), `MERGEABLE`, not draft, 0 reviews. Same repo-class handling — NOT routed to product `*-pr-approver` (NanoClaw-platform fork; no nanoclaw approver wired; `pr_ready_for_review` webhook carried the generic post-#874 "route to *-pr-approver" task string, standing rule overrides — routing a slang/slangpy approver at a nanoclaw-repo agent-runner fix would be nonsensical), NOT commented (clean self-evident maintainer fix + the only-red-check is a documented pre-existing flake, comment hygiene), merge is maintainer's (`nv-main` tracks upstream, outside nv-coworkers auto-merge grant). On redelivery / `synchronize`: no-op unless CI flips a NEW (non-flake) red or a substantive human comment lands. **`synchronize` 2026-07-16** = rebase re-push onto `nv-main` (head `540b870`), diff BYTE-IDENTICAL (+174/-17, 5 files, 2 commits, last commit still commit-2 critical-review fix) — CONTENT unchanged, verdict stands; `check`✓, `ci` pending (re-run, expected — not a new red); pure no-op, not routed/reviewed/commented.

**#961 (2026-07-16)** — `fix(agent-runner): stop test poll-loops leaking across files (fixes the /clear-abort CI flake)`, branch `fix/pollloop-clear-abort-race` → `nv-main`, author **szihs** (human/maintainer), **test-infra + prod-safe seam** (+57/-21, 3 files: `poll-loop.ts`, `integration.test.ts`, `upload-trace.test.ts`). **Root-cause fix for the EXACT pre-existing flake I attributed #960's red `ci` to** — `integration.test.ts › poll loop … aborts the active query when /clear arrives as a follow-up` (`unable to open database file`). **Bug:** the `runPollLoopWithTimeout` test helper (identical in both test files) returned the `Promise.race()` abort/timeout arm and NEVER drained the real `runPollLoop`; `config.signal` was only observed BETWEEN queries (top of outer `while`), never while blocked inside `for await (event of query.events)`, so after `controller.abort()` the race settled but the underlying loop kept its active query open. Bun runs all test files sequentially in ONE shared process → orphan loops kept polling LATER test files' freshly-created DBs (the `unable to open database file`) and could steal the `/clear` row before the active-query test's `BlockingProvider` observed it; `integration.test.ts` sorts late alphabetically so accumulated orphans + dependence on the 500ms `setInterval` firing within a wall-clock budget made it flake under host load. **Fix:** (1) `poll-loop.ts` wires `config.signal → query.abort()` INSIDE `processQuery` (where the `setInterval` lives) via `const onSignalAbort=()=>query.abort()` + `addEventListener('abort',…,{once:true})` + `finally removeEventListener` — an aborted loop now breaks its stream and returns within one iteration; (2) new `if (config.signal?.aborted) return;` before the fallback `markCompleted` — a caller-requested stop is NOT a completed turn, so the processing claim is left for the next loop to reset rather than silently consumed; (3) `activePollIntervalMs?` test seam (defaults to `ACTIVE_POLL_INTERVAL_MS`) lets tests drive follow-up polling at 10ms deterministically; (4) both test helpers drain the real loop after abort (`if (signal.aborted) await loop.catch(()=>{})`). **Inline review verdict: clean, correct, internally consistent, source-verified at head `8e81665`.** (a) `query.abort()` is a REAL pre-existing method — `AgentQuery` iface declares `abort(): void` (providers/types.ts:160), already called at poll-loop.ts:669 for the existing `/clear` path; the PR reuses the exact mechanism, adds signal wiring. (b) Handler `{once:true}` + `finally removeEventListener` → no listener leak. (c) `if (config.signal?.aborted) return;` semantics match its comment 1:1. (d) **PROD IS A PURE NO-OP** — production caller `index.ts:238` `runPollLoop({provider,providerName,cwd,systemContext})` passes NO `signal` and NO `activePollIntervalMs`, so `signal?.addEventListener` skips, `if(config.signal?.aborted)` is falsy→falls through to markCompleted as before, interval stays `ACTIVE_POLL_INTERVAL_MS`; production behavior genuinely unchanged. The PR-body parenthetical ("also correct production shutdown behavior — a killed container no longer runs its in-flight turn to completion") is FORWARD-LOOKING, NOT activated by this PR (prod wires no signal) — harmless overstatement, not a defect. **CI ALL GREEN** — `check`✓ `ci`✓ `label`✓ (run 29478537171); crucially the composed-state `ci` check PASSES, confirming this PR is the fix for the flake that made #960 red. `mergeStateStatus: UNSTABLE`→now-green, `MERGEABLE`, not draft, 0 reviews. **Merge-order note (maintainer's, not a blocker):** #960 (a2a thrown-bounce) also touches `poll-loop.ts`'s outer-catch / fallback-`markCompleted` region on `nv-main`; whichever merges first, the other needs a trivial rebase in that block — both szihs-authored, maintainer sequences. Same repo-class handling — NOT routed to product `*-pr-approver` (NanoClaw-platform fork; no nanoclaw approver wired; `pr_ready_for_review` webhook carried the generic post-#874 "route to *-pr-approver" task string, standing rule overrides), NOT commented (clean self-evident maintainer test-infra fix + all-green CI, comment hygiene), merge is maintainer's (`nv-main` tracks upstream, outside nv-coworkers auto-merge grant). On redelivery / `synchronize`: no-op unless CI flips a NEW red or a substantive human comment lands.

**#962 (2026-07-16)** — `feat(codex-critique): flag comment-noise as must-fix during code review`, branch `fix/codex-comment-hygiene` → `nv-main`, author **szihs** (human/maintainer), **markdown-only** (+1/-0, single file `container/skills/codex-critique/SKILL.md`). **Layer 1 of 3 for #933** (the enforcement half). Adds ONE criterion to the pinned reviewer `developer-instructions` block in the codex-critique skill, inserted between the scope-shrinkage line and `Return ONLY the structured output below.`: a comment that restates the adjacent line, or narrates change-history / scratchpad reasoning / why-an-alternative-was-rejected (PR-body/commit content, not source), is **must-fix**; a concise *why*-comment (intent/invariant/edge-case) is correct and must NOT be flagged, and self-evident code must NOT be forced to carry comments. (c) Stage-scoping via the parenthetical "(when a code diff is under review)" keeps it inert on DIAGNOSIS/PLAN artifacts (no diff), so the shared reviewer block didn't need forking — coherent. (d) Double-guard last sentence ("do NOT flag concise why-comments, do NOT demand comments on self-evident code") is the deliberate anti-over-fire + anti-collision-with-clarity-reviewer (Reviewer C) design; sound. Discovery-side complement to #903 (operator-only docker reclaim). **Fix:** (1) name-agnostic discovery `find prod-groups -mindepth 2 -maxdepth 3 -name .git -type f -not -path '*/.*/*'` → strip `/.git`, `du -sh`, sort; (2) issue-number = first ≥4-digit run in basename (`grep -oE '[0-9]{4,}' | head -1`), no-number → NO-PR wake-to-confirm; (3) owner routing = the tier folder the path lives under (`slang-reviewer/…`→reviewer, not always a fixer); (4) generalized dispatch template `wt-<slug>`/`/workspace/agent/slang`→`<dir>`/`<base-clone>`; added R8 `prunable`-is-not-a-reap-signal caveat. **Inline review verdict: clean, correct, internally consistent.** Verified the load-bearing git fact: a linked worktree's `.git` is a FILE (`gitdir:` pointer), a base clone's `.git` is a DIR → `-name .git -type f` finds exactly worktrees, never base checkouts; `-not -path '*/.*/*'` excludes the `.pr-worktrees` scratch dir; all four cited naming styles resolve via the ≥4-digit extractor; `-mindepth 2 -maxdepth 3` matches `prod-groups/<folder>/<wt>/.git` (depth 3) coherently; `prunable`-over-read-only-mount caveat is an accurate git nuance. No behavior code, no tests (prose-only). **#915 (2026-07-13)** — `feat(supervise-issues): critical-pressure tier — reclaim idle KEEP builds at ENOSPC risk`, branch `nv-gc-critical-tier` → `nv-main`, author **szihs** (human/maintainer), **code+docs** (+83/-5, 3 files: `scripts/worktree-gc.py`, `scripts/test_worktree_gc.py`, `reference.md`). Touches **my own `/supervise-issues` skill** — live-emergency fix for the 2026-07-13 12:10 tick where `/ephemeral` hit 754M free but `worktree-gc.py` reclaimed 0 GB: every 7 GB build was `KEEP` (open PRs touched 1–3d ago, correctly classified), so the STALE-OPEN tier (idle >14d) had no lever. **Fix:** new **CRITICAL tier** — below `CRITICAL_GATE_GB=5` (`summary.critical:true`, ENOSPC-imminent) the build-reclaim pool widens to also include **idle KEEP builds** (open PR touched within 14d but idle > `CRITICAL_IDLE_DAYS=2` AND no running session). Still **build-only** (`rm -rf <dir>/build`) — worktree/branch/PR/uncommitted work survive, cmake rebuilds on resume. Routine 5–25 GB pressure unchanged (STALE-OPEN only). **Inline review verdict: clean, correct, internally consistent.** Verified merged tree at `12b0606b`: (a) `running = set(payload["running_dirs"])` defined; `critical` branch nests inside `under_pressure` (5<25 so always reachable when critical); (b) idle-KEEP filter triple-guards `has_build ∧ dir not in running ∧ (pr_idle_days or 0) > 2`; (c) None-safe sort `pr_idle_days or 0` (matches #886/#901 fixes) → most-idle-first regardless of tier; (d) `classify()` untouched, KEEP still = running OR (OPEN/OPEN ∧ idle ≤14) — the critical tier reclaims a *subset* of KEEP without reclassifying. Ran suite locally at merged tree: **20/20 green** (incl. 4 new `TestCriticalTier`: routine-spares-KEEP / critical-reclaims-idle-KEEP / running-KEEP-never-reclaimed / idle-floor-respected / stale-then-idle-oldest-first). **#916 (2026-07-13)** — `fix(agent-runner): skip git worktrees in additionalDirectories (autocompact thrash)`, branch `nv-fix-additionaldirs-worktree-dup` → `nv-main`, author **szihs** (human/maintainer), **code** (+61/-22, 2 files: `container/agent-runner/src/index.ts`, new `container/agent-runner/src/additional-dirs.test.ts`). **THE ROOT-CAUSE FIX for the fixer-autocompact-thrash class** ([[project_11917_fixer_compaction_loop]]) — #913's peer-review-invariant only mitigated the symptom (Step-8 skips); THIS removes the cause. **Root cause:** `index.ts` added EVERY `/workspace/agent/*` subdir with a `.claude/` to the SDK's `additionalDirectories`; the SDK loads each dir's `.claude/agents/` + `CLAUDE.md`. A writer tier accumulates many git *worktrees* of the same repo (prod slang-fixer: 54 `wt-slang-*`), each carrying upstream Slang's identical `.claude/` (5 reviewer subagents + 33.7KB CLAUDE.md) → repo agents registered ~54× + CLAUDE.md injected ~54×, re-sent every turn as ~69KB `agent_listing_delta`+`nested_memory` that survives compaction → window refills in ~3 turns → thrash. slangpy-fixer (fewer worktrees, smaller CLAUDE.md) didn't thrash on identical config → confirms it's worktree COUNT not config. **Fix:** include a repo's PRIMARY checkout, skip its linked worktrees — a worktree's `.git` is a FILE (gitdir pointer), a primary clone's `.git` is a DIR (same load-bearing git fact as #904/#905). Extracted pure exported `discoverAdditionalDirectories(bases, cwd)`; guarded `main()` with `import.meta.main` so the helper imports without booting the poll loop. Prod effect: slang-fixer additional-dirs 57→3, agent registrations ~270→~5. **Inline review verdict: clean, correct, internally consistent.** Verified the diff: (a) worktree-skip applies ONLY to CWD subdirs (`base === cwd`) that already passed the `.claude/` gate — non-CWD `/workspace/extra` subdirs still included unconditionally (behavior-preserving); (b) `.git` stat wrapped try/except → can't-stat falls through and INCLUDES the dir (safe: a real clone with an odd `.git` isn't wrongly dropped); (c) `import.meta.main` is the correct Bun idiom — container runs `exec bun …src/index.ts` as entrypoint per CLAUDE.md → true there, importable-without-boot in tests; (d) 5 tests cover the real 1-clone+54-worktree→collapses-to-clone, plain `.claude/`-no-`.git` included, no-`.claude/` excluded, non-CWD base includes all. PR body: `bun test` 275 pass / 0 fail. **Deploy note in body (operator task, NOT this PR):** agent-runner src reaches containers via the copy-once per-group overlay (`data/v2-sessions/<gid>/agent-runner-src/`, seeded only when absent — `group-init.ts`), NOT auto-refreshed like the skill mirror → landing needs overlay re-sync into existing groups + container respawn. Same repo-class handling — NOT routed to product `*-pr-approver` (NanoClaw-platform fork; no nanoclaw approver wired; `pr_ready_for_review` webhook carried the generic post-#874 "route to *-pr-approver" task string, standing rule overrides), NOT commented (clean self-evident maintainer fix, hygiene), merge is maintainer's (`nv-main` tracks upstream, outside nv-coworkers auto-merge grant; szihs-owned). CI `check`✓ `label`✓ pass / `ci` pending at review time; `MERGEABLE`, UNSTABLE, not draft, 0 reviews. On redelivery / `synchronize`: no-op unless CI flips red or a substantive human comment lands.

**#917 (2026-07-13)** — `refactor(agent-runner): precise linked-worktree predicate + side-effect-free helper`, branch `nv-additionaldirs-worktree-predicate` → `nv-main`, author **szihs** (human/maintainer), **code** (+ new file; `bun test` 279 pass / 9 new). **Direct follow-up to #916 addressing review feedback** — refines the autocompact-thrash root-cause fix ([[project_11917_fixer_compaction_loop]]). **Two changes:** (1) **Correctness — #916's predicate was too broad.** #916 skipped any CWD subdir whose `.git` is a FILE, but `.git`-is-a-file ALSO matches **submodules** (`gitdir: ../.git/modules/<name>`) and **`--separate-git-dir`** clones — both legitimate distinct checkouts that *should* contribute their `.claude/` skills; #916 wrongly excluded them. New `isLinkedGitWorktree(dir)` (in new `container/agent-runner/src/additional-directories.ts`) reads the `.git` pointer and skips **only** when `gitdir:` contains the `.git/worktrees/` admin segment — the exact `git worktree add` signature (regex `/(^|\/)\.git\/worktrees\//` on separator-normalized gitdir). Submodules + separate-git-dir fall through → kept. (2) **Testability** — discovery extracted from the runtime entrypoint into side-effect-free `additional-directories.ts`; `index.ts` imports `discoverAdditionalDirectories(['/workspace/extra', CWD], CWD)` and drops its now-unused `fs` import; `import.meta.main` guard retained (defense in depth). **Inline review verdict: clean, correct, internally consistent.** Verified full helper + tests + index.ts at head `nv-additionaldirs-worktree-predicate`: (a) predicate is precise — worktree→skip, submodule (`.git/modules/`)→keep, separate-git-dir (arbitrary path)→keep, primary clone (`.git` dir)→keep, no `.git`→keep; (b) **every uncertainty/failure path errs toward INCLUDING** — `statSync` throw→false, `!isFile()`→false, unreadable `readFileSync`→false, no `gitdir:` match→false (same safe-direction property as #916); (c) worktree-skip + `.claude/` gate apply ONLY inside `if (base === cwd)` — non-CWD `/workspace/extra` subdirs still included unconditionally (behavior-preserving from #916); (d) `index.ts` confirmed to use only `path`/`url`, no `fs`; (e) 9 tests match behavior 1:1 incl. real 1-clone+54-worktrees→collapses-to-clone and submodule-with-`.claude/`→kept. Deploy note in body (operator task, NOT this PR): same copy-once overlay path as #916 but adds a NEW file → overlay sync must include `additional-directories.ts` (not just overwrite `index.ts`) into the 13 group overlays + container cycle; ref review-note #5 (stale copy-once overlay is its own bug) filed as a mental follow-up (versioned/mtime `agent-runner-src` refresh). Same repo-class handling — NOT routed to product `*-pr-approver` (NanoClaw-platform fork; no nanoclaw approver wired; `pr_ready_for_review` webhook carried the generic post-#874 "route to *-pr-approver" task string, standing rule overrides), NOT commented (clean self-evident maintainer refactor, hygiene), merge is maintainer's (`nv-main` tracks upstream, outside nv-coworkers auto-merge grant; szihs-owned). **#918 (2026-07-13)** — `feat(agent-runner): refresh primary clones at boot (ff-only + submodules)`, branch `nv-boot-fetch-primary-clones` → `nv-main`, author **szihs** (human/maintainer), **code** (+170/-0, 3 files: `container/agent-runner/src/index.ts`, new `refresh-clones.ts` +111, new `refresh-clones.test.ts` +51). **Direct follow-on to #916/#917** (autocompact-thrash worktree-dedup) — now that the SDK loads `.claude/agents/`+`CLAUDE.md` from the PRIMARY clone only (worktrees skipped), a STALE primary clone means outdated agent defs/project memory + worktrees branched off a base behind `origin` (observed: prod slang-fixer's slang-rhi clone 34 days stale). **Fix:** `refreshPrimaryClones()` at container boot iterates the discovered primary clones: `git pull --ff-only --quiet` (NOT bare fetch — a plain fetch only advances remote-tracking refs, leaving the working-tree files the SDK reads stale; NOT `reset --hard` — would clobber base state; `--ff-only` fetches AND fast-forwards the tree, no-op on diverged/dirty base) → `git submodule update --init --recursive` (slang/slang-rhi carry submodules). Guards: `shouldRefresh` 30-min TTL via FETCH_HEAD mtime (a group-restart boots 15+ containers sharing ONE clone — first refreshes, rest no-op, no remote-storm); best-effort try/catch (offline/token/non-FF/detached-HEAD logged not fatal); bounded timeouts (120s pull / 300s submodule). Pure helpers `shouldRefresh`/`isPrimaryClone`/`FETCH_TTL_MS` exported for tests. **Inline review verdict: clean, correct, internally consistent.** Verified: (a) `isPrimaryClone` — `.git` dir→true, worktree `.git`-file→false, absent→false (same git fact as #904/#905/#916/#917); (b) `shouldRefresh` null→true / within-TTL→false / older→true / `>=` boundary→true, tests 1:1; (c) `--ff-only` genuinely no-ops on diverged/dirty (non-zero exit→caught), never merges/clobbers; (d) worktree-safe (FF of base branch can't touch independent worktree checkouts; git forbids same branch in two checkouts); (e) best-effort try/catch + bounded timeouts → boot never blocks; body: `bun test` 286 pass / 7 new. **ONE non-blocking cosmetic NIT:** the call-site comment in `index.ts` reads `FETCH-ONLY (never pull/checkout …)` — STALE wording contradicting the actual `git pull --ff-only` and the PR body's own (correct) pull-over-fetch argument (per body, the fetch→pull change came from review feedback; the call-site comment wasn't updated). Code behavior is right; only the leftover comment misleads. Same repo-class handling — NOT routed to product `*-pr-approver` (NanoClaw-platform fork; no nanoclaw approver wired; `pr_ready_for_review` webhook carried the generic post-#874 "route to *-pr-approver" task string, standing rule overrides), NOT commented (clean self-evident maintainer feature PR, green, hygiene — same as #912–#917; the stale-comment nit is cosmetic, maintainer-owned, not worth a nag on their own green PR), merge is maintainer's (`nv-main` tracks upstream, outside nv-coworkers auto-merge grant; szihs-owned). Deploy note in body (operator task, NOT this PR): same copy-once overlay path as #916/#917 — sync `refresh-clones.ts`/`refresh-clones.test.ts`/`index.ts` into the 13 group overlays + container respawn. CI `check`✓ `ci`✓ `label`✓ ALL GREEN; `MERGEABLE`, `mergeStateStatus: CLEAN`, not draft, 0 reviews. On redelivery / `synchronize`: no-op unless CI flips red or a substantive human comment lands.

**#920 (2026-07-13)** — `fix(slang-mcp): forward Discord follow-ups from the always-on daemon`, branch `fix/nv-slang-bot/discord-followup-daemon-forward` → `nv-slang`, author **nv-slang-bot[bot]**, **code** (+282/-16, 3 files: `slang-mcp/src/discord/discord.py` +29/-3, `feedback_collector.py` +120/-13, new `test/test_discord_followup_forward.py` +133). **Root-cause fix for intermittent Discord OP-follow-up drops.** The follow-up forwarder (`on_message`→POST dashboard-ingress) lived ONLY in `slang-mcp/discord.py`, whose Discord Gateway is per-MCP-session and reaped after ~10 min idle by `supergateway --stateful` (child killed) → forwarding worked only while a container's MCP session was warm; cold windows dropped follow-ups (verified via `thread_state.jsonl` gaps, e.g. 07-09→07-13). **Fix:** make the always-on `feedback_collector.py` daemon (permanent Gateway, own systemd service) the canonical forwarder. **Inline review verdict: clean, correct, internally consistent.** Verified all load-bearing claims at head: (1) **ownership axis is a correct mirror-image** — both gates key on `DISCORD_POST_SUMMON`: `discord.py._forward_followups_disabled()` = `env!="1"` (default→disabled=daemon owns; `=1`→enabled=slang-mcp owns), `feedback_collector._forward_followups_here()` = `env!="1"` after a `DISCORD_READ_ONLY=1` hard-off → exactly one forwards (prod→daemon / lego→slang-mcp), no duplicate public reply when both Gateways momentarily warm; (2) **cap pre-count claim true** — slang-mcp `SummonView.get_help` ALREADY does `summoned`+`bot_reply` (discord.py 304-305); PR adds the matching pair to daemon `SummonView` (203, 207) so shared `thread_state.jsonl` bot_reply accounting agrees across both processes; (3) **thread_id passthrough** — `_post_to_dashboard(content, thread_id)` adds `body["thread_id"]` only when truthy (both files), passed on summon + follow-up paths, omitted when None→group catch-all; routes each thread to its own per-thread session (wiring `session_mode=per-thread`); (4) daemon `on_message` **audits FIRST** (`_save_thread_reply` unconditional) then gates OP-only/summoned/resolved/cap (mirrors slang-mcp), pre-records bot_reply before POST (race guard); the `state.bot_reply_count - 1` log correctly recovers pre-increment (daemon `state` is the LIVE defaultdict mutated by `_record_thread_event`, unlike discord.py's dict-snapshot which logs pre-value directly — both log pre-value, consistent, not a bug). 8 new tests cover both gates (exactly-one-forwarder prod+lego), thread_id passthrough (present+None), summon pre-count; body: slang-mcp suite 61 pass/5 skip, 4 fails pre-existing on baseline nv-slang (test_config env + test_discord send-allowlist, verified by stash). **Deploy note in body (operator task, NOT this PR):** inert until `systemctl --user restart nanoclaw-prod-discord-feedback.service` (brief Gateway drop — do when forum quiet). Closes a vocabulary-drift gap: the slang/slangpy `*-pr-approver` coworker types **require** `[DECISION_REVIEW, OUTPUT_REVIEW]` and the on-deliver critique gate blocks their decision until both have a recorded round, but the `codex-critique` SKILL.md (the skill the approver opens to *construct* a critique call) listed only `DIAGNOSIS_REVIEW|PLAN_REVIEW|CODE_REVIEW|OUTPUT_REVIEW` — `DECISION_REVIEW` appeared nowhere, so an approver satisfying its gate had to invent how to run the stage it's required to record. The approver `coworker-types.yaml` files that declare the `[DECISION_REVIEW, OUTPUT_REVIEW]` requirement live on the `nv-slang`/`nv-slangpy` overlay branches (composed at CI-merge time), consistent with the fork's overlay model — body's reference accurate. **#936 (2026-07-14) — DASHBOARD CLASS (sibling of #922)** — `fix(dashboard): make Context histogram respect the 1d/7d/30d/all period selector`, branch `nv-dashboard-context-period` → `nv-dashboard`, author **nv-slang-bot[bot]**, **code, server-only** (+152/-57, one file `dashboard/server.ts`). **Root cause:** the Overview "By Coworker" table's Context column (per-session peak-context histogram + mean peak % + compaction `⟳`) was served from a single period-BLIND all-time cache (`contextStatsCache.get(groupId)` → one `ContextStats`; `refreshContextStatsCache` folded every session's all-time peak into one histogram ignoring line timestamps), so it showed all-time numbers regardless of the 1d/7d/30d/all selector while the cost/token columns beside it filtered by period — silent disagreement. **Fix (server-only, no frontend change):** (1) `scanFileContext` now buckets each transcript's signal (`assistant` usage + `compact_boundary`) by the line's own ISO timestamp day via new `isoDayKey(ts)` (`ts.slice(0,10).replace(/-/g,'')` → `YYYYMMDD`, no `Date` parse) into a `PerFileContext.days: Map<YYYYMMDD, DayContextAgg>` (replaces the old flat `peak/compactions/auto/manual/preTokensSum` fields); per-day granularity reconstructs any day-window without re-reading the append-only mtime-cached file. (2) `refreshContextStatsCache` computes the four cutoffs once via `ccusageSinceDate(0/7/30)` + `''` for all, then folds each file's per-day map into all four periods in one pass; a session with no in-window day drops out of that period. (3) `contextStatsCache` value type widened flat `ContextStats` → `Record<ContextPeriod,ContextStats|null>`; new `buildContextStats(acc, maxContext, capped)` (math mirrors the original single-period build; returns null on 0 sessions). (4) endpoint picks `rec[ctxPeriod] ?? rec.all ?? null`, `ctxPeriod` narrowed from the `period` query param (which is typed over ccusageCache keys incl. `lastRefresh`) via `CONTEXT_PERIODS.includes(...)` default-all. **Inline review verdict: clean, correct, internally consistent.** Verified the ONE load-bearing correctness claim at head `nv-dashboard-context-period` — that the Context windows agree with the cost columns *by construction*: `ccusageSinceDate` returns `YYYYMMDD` (server.ts:1921 `toISOString().slice(0,10).replace(/-/g,'')`); the cost path's `within` (server.ts:2416) does `entry.date.replace(/-/g,'') >= since` LEXICALLY; the new Context path (server.ts:1747) does `key >= since` where `key = isoDayKey = ts.slice(0,10).replace(/-/g,'')` — identical big-endian `YYYYMMDD` lexical `>=` against the identical cutoffs → same boundaries. Also verified: (a) `MISSING_TS_KEY='00000000'` (timestamp-less/invalid signal) never satisfies a real cutoff so lands ONLY in `all` (dated periods gated by `period !== 'all' && !(key >= since)`); (b) `maxContext` (`groupMaxWindow || modelMaxContext('unknown')`) folded once at file level → fixed % scale/histogram bucketing across periods, only session membership changes when toggling; (c) all FIVE `contextStatsCache` refs (decl 1593, two `.delete` 1705/1777, `.set` 1780, endpoint read 7159) match the new `Record` shape — no stale flat-shape caller; (d) `!rec.all` delete guard = the old `sessions===0` condition (all = superset); (e) no frontend change needed — period buttons already re-fetch (`app.js:loadMetricsTokens`), `contextCellHtml` already renders `—` for null. Body verification (tsc clean, `dashboard/server.test.ts` 78/78, prod-transcript replay: periods differ e.g. Orchestrator 1d=40 vs all=400 sessions, maxContext fixed across periods, counts monotonic 1d≤7d≤30d≤all, old-only groups → null → `—`) consistent with the diff. Same repo-class handling — NOT routed to product `*-pr-approver` (NanoClaw-platform fork; no nanoclaw approver wired; `pr_ready_for_review` webhook reason=opened carried the generic post-#874 "route to *-pr-approver" task string, standing rule overrides — routing a slang/slangpy approver at a nanoclaw dashboard PR is nonsensical, same as #922), NOT commented (clean self-evident maintainer/bot fix, hygiene), merge is maintainer's (`nv-dashboard` outside nv-coworkers auto-merge grant). **#937 (2026-07-14) — DASHBOARD CLASS (sibling of #922/#936)** — `fix(dashboard): spawn ccusage directly (node cli.js), not via npx — kill the fan-out pileup`, branch `nv-dashboard-ccusage-direct-spawn` → `nv-dashboard`, author **nv-slang-bot[bot]**, **code, server-only** (+107/-25, one file `dashboard/server.ts`). **Root-cause fix for a REAL prod incident today (~13:30 UTC):** dashboard cost panel wedged the whole server — systemd `active (running)`, node held :3737, but every HTTP request hung (listen-socket `Recv-Q` piling, one worker thread pinned ~64% CPU >1h); cgroup full of stacked `npx ccusage codex daily` subprocesses. **Cause:** `ccusage` is NOT a declared dep → every call is `npx ccusage …`, re-running npm exec resolution (`sh -c npx`→`npm exec ccusage`→node) PER CALL (measured npx 2.48s vs direct node 0.73s, ~1.7s tax). One refresh fans out ~30–56; under host load the fan-out outran the 60s refresh interval, batches stacked, process count spiralled, event loop wedged. Prior fixes #484 (subprocess cleanup)/#488 (bound fan-out)/#617 (re-entrancy+concurrency cap) all capped the *number* of calls, never removed the *per-call npm-exec tax* → kept recurring. **Fix:** new memoized `resolveCcusageCli()` — scans `~/.npm/_npx/*/node_modules/ccusage/src/cli.js`, picks most-recent mtime (version bumps win), warms once via `npx --yes ccusage --version` (execSync, 120s timeout) on cold cache then re-scans; tri-state cache sentinel (`null`=unresolved / `''`=resolved-failed→npx fallback / non-empty=path). Both `runCcusage` (Claude) + `runCodexCcusage` (Codex) pivot `proc = cli ? execFile(process.execPath, [cli, ...ccusageArgs], opts, cb) : exec('npx ccusage …', opts, cb)` — direct `node <cli.js>` (one process, no shell, no npm exec) when resolved, npx fallback otherwise; callback/timeout/env logic unchanged. **Inline review verdict: clean, correct, internally consistent.** Verified at head `nv-dashboard-ccusage-direct-spawn`: (a) all new-code imports present in the edited block — `execFile`+`execSync` (child_process), `homedir` (os), `existsSync`/`readdirSync`/`statSync` (fs), `join` (path) all imported (`execFile`+`homedir` newly added by the diff) → no build break; (b) `findCli()` guards `readdirSync`/`statSync` in try/catch → returns `''` on any FS failure, never throws; (c) **no warm-up storm** — `resolveCcusageCli()` is called SYNCHRONOUSLY inside each `new Promise()` executor (executors run sync at construction), and the fan-out builds the promise array left-to-right, so the FIRST `runCcusage()` blocks on the one-time execSync warm-up and every subsequent construction sees the populated cache → exactly one warm-up per process, not per call; (d) fallback-to-npx on unresolved CLI = cost reporting degrades not breaks (fix's stated safety property); (e) `--offline` + `CCUSAGE_MODEL_ALIASES` (Claude) + `CODEX_HOME` (codex) env preserved; the actual fix = memoizing the resolve so the ~1.7s tax is paid once, not 30–56× per refresh. Body verification (esbuild transform passes = same tsx runtime transform, no new type errors in edited region, live-npx-cache run resolves cli.js + valid daily JSON ~0.2s + memoized 2nd resolve 0ms) consistent with the diff. **#943 (2026-07-15)** — `fix(a2a): redrive bounced handoffs + mechanical supervisor enforcement`, branch `nv-a2a-redrive` → `nv-main`, author **nv-slang-bot[bot]**, **code** (+1170/-46, 18 files). **THE root-cause fix for the [[project_12097_ser_spirv14_vs_12099]] stuck-handoff incident** (a2a triage handoff for slang#12097 delivered during the ~20-min [[project_slang_fixer_auth_outage]], recipient turn errored `Not logged in · Please run /login`, trigger acked **completed**, never re-driven; 12h cron flagged `needs_nudge=true` but the LLM parked it behind free-text `"auth-blocked; queued"`). Two independent bugs fixed, touches core a2a infra + **my own `/supervise-issues` skill**. **Bug A (container, belt):** new `transient-error.ts` `classifyTurnError` (allowlist-driven, permanent-WINS, NOT fail-open) → transient|unknown|permanent; on `isError && channelType==='agent' && !permanent`, `poll-loop` calls `markBounced` (distinct `processing_ack` status `bounced-transient`/`bounced-unknown`) INSTEAD of `completed`, does NOT relay the auth blip to the peer, and threads `bouncedIds` out of `processQuery` so the outer fallback `markCompleted` filters them (closes the "handoff permanently consumed" hole). `syncProcessingAcks` ignores the new statuses → trigger `messages_in` stays `pending`. **Bug B (host, suspenders):** `host-sweep.redriveBouncedA2a` — `!alive`-gated (host = sole outbound.db writer, mirrors resetStuckProcessingRows), re-arms the pending trigger with outage-scale backoff (`A2A_MAX_TRIES=12`, 1→60min capped 1h for transient; `A2A_UNKNOWN_MAX_TRIES=2` fast dead-letter for unknown) via `retryWithBackoff`, else `markMessageFailed` + dead-letter (notify live delegator session, else escalate to admin as a CHAT not an approval gate). New session-db helpers `getBouncedClaims`/`deleteBouncedClaims`(id-specific, never blanket)/`getBouncedTriggerRow`. **Supervisor:** `scan.py` emits `action` strict-1:1 with `needs_nudge` (**no `suppress` escape hatch**), enum `non_nudge_reason`, `summary.must_nudge`; SKILL.md §3 becomes a **fails-loudly** `sent_nudges != must_nudge` → `[SUPERVISOR INVARIANT VIOLATION]` + `ask_user_question` (makes "never suppress a nudge row" CHECKABLE — the exact #901 prose-only gap that was violated by #12097); additive stopped+errored bounce limb in `we_owe_next_step`/`any_stopped_errored`; `pull-universe.sh` rehydrates `disposition` from prior state (fixes HUMAN_OWNED-gate-sees-None over-flag, the Tick-86 105→1 noise) + new `--reverse` ncl flag keeps newest outbound's text for `last_outbound_error_class` (py mirror of `transient-error.ts`, kept in sync by comment). **Inline review verdict: clean, correct, well-tested.** Verified load-bearing claims in the diff: (a) bounce ordering is correct — re-arm (future `process_after` + `tries++`) FIRST, clear marker AFTER (else re-fires with no backoff); (b) idempotency guard `processAfter > now` leaves future-scheduled bounces alone (does NOT push to `handled`); (c) `tries` accumulates across redrive cycles — `retryWithBackoff` persists on messages_in, `markBounced` (INSERT OR REPLACE) doesn't touch tries; (d) `classifyTurnError` permanent-wins verified (`503 then billing_error`→permanent); (e) py `classify_error_text` guards false-positives (a normal reply mentioning "login" → None, no spurious nudge — `test_normal_reply_mentioning_login`); (f) `--reverse` = `b.seq-a.seq` then `slice(offset, offset+limit)` correctly returns newest N (plain `--limit 1` returned OLDEST). Tests: scan.py 31 · pull-universe 13 (py) · transient-error 8 · poll-loop bounce 5 (bun) · host-sweep redrive 7 (vitest, injected DB + dead-letter spy); body: host vitest 1369 pass, container bun green, both tsc clean. **CI ALL GREEN** — `ci`✓ (composed build+test PASSED — the authoritative composed-state gate that tripped #928/#930/#942 red; green here means all new imports incl. `getSourceFor`@`db/a2a-session-sources.js` + session-db exports resolve/typecheck in the composed tree) `check`✓ nv-* path guard `label`✓; `MERGEABLE`, UNSTABLE→green, not draft, 0 reviews. **Deploy note in body (operator task, NOT this PR):** `./container/build.sh` (poll-loop baked) + refresh mounted skill for running containers — same copy-once overlay caveat as #916/#917/#918. (2) `session-hook.ts`: `MEMORY_SESSION_HOOK` = cmd `bun /app/src/memory/hook.ts`, legacy `['bun /app/src/memory-hook.ts']`, sources `[startup,clear,compact]`; `memoryContextForSessionStart` returns undefined for `resume` else renders — matcher EXCLUDES resume (no re-inject on resume, consistent). (3) `hook.ts` reads stdin JSON, validates `source` against `[...sources,'resume']`, FAILS CLOSED (`{}`/`{not-json` → empty stdout) — test-confirmed; no argv[2] in the wired cmd → baseDir defaults `/workspace/agent`. (5) **`claude.ts writeMemorySessionHook` is the key host-integration piece + is idempotent** — reads/validates `settings.json`→`hooks`→`SessionStart` (each type-guarded, throws on non-object/non-array), builds `memoryCommands = {command, ...legacy}`, maps existing entries through `removeMemoryCommands` (strips any hook whose `command` is in the set, drops the entry if its `hooks[]` empties), then pushes `{matcher:'startup|clear|compact', hooks:[{type:'command',command,timeout:10}]}` → re-running strips prior/legacy before re-adding, so repeated boots don't stack duplicate hooks. Matcher `startup|clear|compact` = Claude Code SessionStart regex over `source`, resume excluded (hook.ts ALSO guards resume = belt+suspenders). Content: `SendMessage` moves from `TOOL_ALLOWLIST` into `SDK_DISALLOWED_TOOLS`, both consts gain `export`, + new `claude.tool-collisions.test.ts` (32 lines) + CHANGELOG entry. **Fix is sound**: the SDK built-in addresses Claude Code's own in-session subagents, so it shadowed `mcp__nanoclaw__send_message`; the `preToolUseHook` (`claude.ts:239`) already blocks anything in `SDK_DISALLOWED_TOOLS`, so listing it there is the whole fix — verified at head, no new enforcement code needed.

⭐⭐⭐**THE FINDING — CI attempt 1 FAILED and attempt 2 PASSED on the IDENTICAL head `e612f89f`, and it is NOT a flake: it is a genuine cross-branch ORDERING race in the composed-state merge, which resolved itself when its sibling PR merged 4 minutes later.** Failure was `Container tests` → `SyntaxError: Export named 'TOOL_ALLOWLIST' not found in module '…/providers/claude.ts'` (346 pass / 1 fail; the NEW test file was the only casualty). Mechanism, read out of both attempts' `Merge nv-* branches` step logs:
- `ci.yml` builds a **composed state** — it merges all sibling `nv-*` branches, and auto-resolves any conflict inside nv-main's owned set (`container/agent-runner/*` is owned) by `git checkout origin/nv-main -- <f>`.
- The PR's new test `import`s `TOOL_ALLOWLIST` from `claude.ts`; the export only exists on the synced code. **Attempt 1 (14:24Z): `claude.ts` CONFLICTED against `origin/nv-main` and was resolved to nv-main's copy — which at that moment was `c0bc5a03`, still `const` not `export const` (verified: `c0bc5a03` has bare `const` at :83, and the test file 404s at that ref).** So the test survived while the module it imports from got overwritten with a version lacking the export ⇒ `SyntaxError`, not a test assertion failure.
- **Attempt 2 (14:28Z): sibling PR #1086 (`sync/upstream-nv-main` → `nv-main`) had merged at 14:27:43Z**, so `origin/nv-main` now carried the same upstream commit. `claude.ts` no longer appears in attempt 2's conflict list at all (confirmed: attempt-2 log shows `.github/workflows/ci.yml` + `package.json` conflicting for nv-main, **no `claude.ts`**) ⇒ green, 17/17 steps success.
- ⚠️**So "re-run made it pass" is TRUE but the reason is NOT transience — the WORLD changed between attempts** (a sibling branch merged). ⭐⭐⭐**A retry that passes because an external ref advanced is indistinguishable in the check-runs API from a flake retry; only the two attempts' merge-step logs discriminate.** Fetch them via `gh api …/actions/runs/<id>/attempts/{1,2}/logs` → unzip; the check-runs endpoint only ever shows the LATEST attempt's conclusion.
- **This is a structural property of the fan-out sync, not a one-off:** `sync-upstream.sh` opens one PR per overlay branch from the same upstream commit, and every leaf PR whose diff touches an nv-main-**owned** path is red until the `nv-main` PR merges. The PR body even states the intended order (*"Composes against the freshly-synced nv-main (PR #1086, merged first)"*) — #1086 merged at 14:27:43Z, i.e. **3m42s AFTER this PR's first CI run started at 14:24:01Z.** Nothing enforces the order; CI just has to be re-run afterwards. Not filed as a bug — no receipts that the maintainer considers it one, and the bot's own retry absorbed it.

**Not commented on GitHub** (comment hygiene, consistent with the whole #874 lineage: bot-authored automated sync, self-merged, clean, nothing substantive to add). **Not escalated** — no auth failure, no task of mine stalled; the red→green was the bot's own retry. On redelivery: **no-op, PR is MERGED** (`state=closed`, `merged=true`, head ref deleted). Verified the merged `nv-slangpy` tip carries the fix (`export const SDK_DISALLOWED_TOOLS` :88, `'SendMessage'` :94, test file present, 1448 B).

⚠️**One measurement caveat, self-caught:** my "`SendMessage` appears nowhere else in the repo" check ran `grep` over the LOCAL clone at `/workspace/agent/nanoclaw-kb`, which is on **`nv-main` @ `c0bc5a03`** — NOT this PR's branch. It is a fact about that tree, not about `nv-slangpy`. (`gh api search/code` returned 0 for `TOOL_ALLOWLIST` and `SDK_DISALLOWED_TOOLS` too — both known-present strings ⇒ **code search is a FALSE ZERO on this repo, do not use it here**; the positive control is what caught it.) The claim that matters was checked directly instead: `claude.ts` fetched at head + at the merged tip.

1. `gh api repos/…/commits/<branch-name>/check-runs` → **HTTP 422 `No commit found for SHA: docs/changelog-nv-2026-08-09`** — the branch was **deleted on merge** (`git/ref/heads/…` → 404), so the ref-style path fails. Resolve `headRefOid` first (`5d36b46a…`) — the SHA route works post-deletion and returned `label ✓ success` (1 check run). ⭐**A 422 "No commit found for SHA" on a *branch name* means the ref is gone, NOT that the name was malformed** — same error string as a fabricated SHA (cf. [[feedback_shallow_clone_makes_your_head_the_graft_root]], [[feedback_a_field_named_like_a_state_is_not_a_test_for_that_state]]).
